病毒 跨物种溢出 的驱动因素:对病原体传播方式的严格检查

Drivers of Viral Spillover: A Critical Examination of How Pathogens Spread
作者： Elenoire Sole, Giulia Montalbano, Giuseppe Motta, Maria Maddalena Pansera, Angelina Midiri, Mariarita Iacopino, Paolo Liotta, Giuseppe Mancuso, Carmelo Biondo
摘要中文翻译由于人们对禽流感、亨德拉病毒、猴痘、尼帕病毒和蝙蝠冠状病毒等病毒威胁的日益担忧,人畜共患病毒 跨物种溢出(从动物传染给人类的病毒 传播)的出现引起了全世界的关注。显然,这些事件与自然发生的事件有很大偏差,它们是生态、环境和社会因素的综合体,深刻地改变了动物和人类领域之间的界限。因此,为了了解人畜共患病毒 跨物种溢出 的当代和未来驱动因素,协调一致、全面的全球“同一个健康”应对措施至关重要。本综述的目的是分析动物 储存宿主 和人类之间 传播 的普遍通路。这项检查涉及对历史病例的分析,包括 SARS 和埃博拉病毒的病例,以及最近发生的事件,例如 SARS-CoV-2 (Covid-19) 的全球大流行。本综述的目的是了解跨物种 传播 的基本机制。制定有效策略来减少人畜共患病毒的出现并预防未来的流行病取决于对这些机制的深入了解。该分析对于识别大流行病原体和防止人畜共患病毒传播至关重要。总之,该审查对当前防止人畜共患病毒传播的全球战略进行了全面评估,突显了我们理解上的差距和需要进一步研究的领域。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注病毒人畜共患病毒 跨物种溢出,特别检查动物 储存宿主 和人类之间的 传播 通路,核心问题是如何理解和减轻 猴痘 等人畜共患病毒的出现。
证据基础与方法
该综述对历史和近期病毒 跨物种溢出 病例进行了全面分析,包括 SARS、埃博拉和 SARS-CoV-2,但没有具体说明数据库、搜索日期或研究计数。
共识与结论
共识是,协调一致的全球“同一个健康”应对措施对于理解和解决人畜共患病毒 跨物种溢出 的驱动因素至关重要,重点关注跨物种 传播 机制。
争议与缺口
该评论强调了对当前防止人畜共患病毒传播的全球战略的理解差距,并确定了进一步研究的领域,但没有详细说明具体的争议。
科研与实践启示
减少人畜共患病毒出现和预防未来大流行的有效策略取决于对跨物种 传播 机制的深入了解,这对大流行病原体的识别和预防具有重要意义。

Drivers of Viral Spillover: A Critical Examination of How Pathogens Spread

Authors: Elenoire Sole, Giulia Montalbano, Giuseppe Motta, Maria Maddalena Pansera, Angelina Midiri, Mariarita Iacopino, Paolo Liotta, Giuseppe Mancuso, Carmelo Biondo
Original abstract (English)
The occurrence of viral zoonotic spillover (the transmission of viruses from animals to humans) has attracted worldwide attention due to mounting concerns regarding viral threats such as avian influenza, Hendra, monkeypox, Nipah and bat coronaviruses. It is evident that these events deviate significantly from natural occurrences, being the conflation of ecological, environmental and social factors that are profoundly altering the boundaries between the animal and human domains. Consequently, in order to comprehend both the contemporary and prospective drivers of zoonotic viral spillover, a coordinated, comprehensive global One Health response is essential. The objective of this review is to analyse prevalent pathways of transmission between animal reservoirs and human populations. This examination involves the analysis of historical cases, including those of SARS and Ebola, as well as recent occurrences, such as the global pandemic of SARS-CoV-2 (Covid-19). The objective of this review is to comprehend the fundamental mechanisms of cross-species transmission. The development of effective strategies to mitigate the emergence of zoonotic viruses and prevent future pandemics is contingent on a robust understanding of these mechanisms. This analysis is key to identifying pandemic pathogens and preventing the spread of zoonotic viruses. In conclusion, the review provides a thorough evaluation of current global strategies to prevent the spread of zoonotic viruses, highlighting gaps in our understanding and areas for further research.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on viral zoonotic spillover, specifically examining transmission pathways between animal reservoirs and human populations, with the central question being how to understand and mitigate the emergence of zoonotic viruses like mpox.
Evidence base and method
The review is a comprehensive analysis of historical and recent viral spillover cases, including SARS, Ebola, and SARS-CoV-2, but does not specify databases, search dates, or study counts.
Consensus and conclusions
The consensus is that a coordinated global One Health response is essential to comprehend and address the drivers of zoonotic viral spillover, with a focus on cross-species transmission mechanisms.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights gaps in understanding current global strategies to prevent zoonotic virus spread and identifies areas for further research, though specific controversies are not detailed.
Research and practice implications
Effective strategies to mitigate zoonotic virus emergence and prevent future pandemics depend on a robust understanding of cross-species transmission mechanisms, with implications for pandemic pathogen identification and prevention.

猴痘:亚利桑那州报告涉及新型病毒的病例 - FOX 10 Phoenix

新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认该州首例 猴痘 病例,涉及 Clade I,这是该病毒的一种更严重的变种。该患者是在美国境外旅行时感染的,目前没有证据表明当地存在 传播。与进化枝 II 相比,进化枝 I 的 死亡率 率更高(1-10%),主要通过动物接触、家庭接触和亲密接触传播。截至 7 月 21 日,亚利桑那州已报告 34 例 猴痘 病例。卫生官员建议在接触后 4 天内使用 猴痘,以获得最大效果,但如果在 14 天内服用,仍可能减少疾病。建议公众在潜在接触后监测症状 21 天。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox: Arizona reports case involving a new type of the virus - FOX 10 Phoenix

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first case of Mpox involving Clade I, a more severe variant of the virus. The patient acquired the infection while traveling outside the U.S., and there is no evidence of local transmission. Clade I, which has a higher mortality rate (1-10%) compared to Clade II, is primarily transmitted through animal exposure, household contacts, and intimate contact. Arizona has reported 34 Mpox cases as of July 21. Health officials recommend vaccination within 4 days of exposure for maximum effectiveness, though it may still reduce illness if administered within 14 days. The public is advised to monitor symptoms for 21 days after potential exposure.

PHOENIX - Health officials in Arizona say they have confirmed a case of Mpox (formerly Monkeypox) that involves a different type of the same virus. What we know: In a statement released on July 22, officials with the Arizona Department of Health Services said the case involved a type of Mpox that is classified as "Clade I." They say this is the first time Arizona has seen a case of confirmed Clade I Mpox. "The individual acquired the infection while traveling outside the country," read a portion of the statement. Dig deeper: According to a factsheet published by the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health, Clade I is one of two main types of Mpox. The factsheet notes identified Clade II as the one that caused an outbreak in the U.S. The factsheet states that Clade I Mpox is "more likely to cause severe illness and death, especially in people who are immunocompromised." Officials say Clade I Mpox is "mostly transmitted through exposure to animals who carry the disease," while also stating that it can be transmitted through household contexts close sexual or intimate contact, and contaminated objects (fomites). In comparison, the factsheet states that Clade II of Mpox is mostly transmitted through same-sex sexual contact. As for the mortality rate for the virus, the factsheet states it ranges from 1% to 10% for Clade I, and from less than 1% to 4% for Clade II. Local perspective: In their statement, AZDHS officials say there are 34 reported cases of Mpox in Arizona as of July 21, which includes the Clade I case. "There is currently no evidence of local transmission connected to this case," officials wrote. What you can do: The factsheet from Johns Hopkins states that two doses of the JYNNEOS vaccines are "expected to protect against both Clade I and Clade II infections." Meanwhile, Arizona health officials say people should contact their healthcare provider or the local health department as soon as possible if they wer exposed to someone with Mpox, noting that vaccination can happen after exposure. "Vaccination is most effective when given within four days of exposure but may still reduce illness when given within 14 days. Check for symptoms for 21 days after your last contact." The Source: Information for this article was gathered from a statement released by the Arizona Department of Public Health, and from a factsheet published by the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health. News Brief
Arizona health officials have announced the state's first travel-associated case of Clade I mpox, a strain not commonly found in the U.S. The infected individual contracted the virus while traveling abroad, and health authorities are now monitoring and following up with potentially exposed people. As of July 21, Arizona has reported 34 mpox cases this year, including both Clade I and Clade II strains. While no local transmission has been confirmed yet, officials emphasize that both strains cause similar symptoms and recommend vaccination for those at risk. The JYNNEOS vaccine can help prevent mpox and reduce severe illness if administered within 14 days of exposure.

KochPublished: Jul. 22, 2026 at 11:49 PM UTCShareAdd Us On GoogleAdd as a preferred source on GooglePHOENIX (AZFamily) — Arizona health officials announced Wednesday that they are tracking the state’s first travel-associated case of a unique mpox strain. The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) said it was notified this month of a state resident who contracted Clade I mpox while traveling outside the country.ADHS and other local public health officials are now monitoring and following up with people who may have been exposed. The department added that there is no evidence of local transmission at this time related to this case. Mpox has two strains: Clade I and Clade II. The latter has been spreading in Arizona and the United States since 2022, ADHS said. Clade I is usually found in parts of Africa and is not common in the U.S., ADHS said. Both strains cause similar symptoms, and officials recommend the same treatment methods: testing, vaccination, clinical management and public health response. The department said Arizona has seen an increase in reported mpox cases this summer. As of July 21, the state had 34 reported cases — both strains included — this year. What is mpox?According to ADHS, mpox is an infection caused by the monkeypox virus. It mainly spreads through close personal contact with someone who has mpox, like the following: Direct skin-to-skin contact with a rash, sores, scabs or body fluidsClose physical contactSharing bedding, towels, clothing or other personal itemsProlonged close contact with someone who has mpoxThe infection can pass to a baby during pregnancy Casual contact in places such as stores, offices or airplanes is unlikely to spread mpoxIf exposed, symptoms may include a new or unexplained rash that looks like pimples or blisters and may be painful or itchy. Some people may also experience fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, chills, exhaustion or respiratory symptoms.People with weaker immune systems may be at greater risk of severe illness from mpox. What can you do? Get vaccinated if you think you may be at risk. If you recently had close physical contact with someone who has mpox, you have an increased risk of contracting the disease. ADHS said the JYNNEOS vaccine can help prevent mpox and reduce the risk of severe illness.If you were exposed to someone with mpox, contact a healthcare provider or local health department as soon as possible. You can get vaccinated after an exposure. Vaccination is most effective when given within four days of exposure but may still reduce illness when given within 14 days. Check for symptoms for 21 days after your last contact. If you develop symptoms, avoid close contact with others, isolate at home and contact your healthcare provider for testing. Tell your provider about any recent travel and other possible exposure to mpox.See a spelling or grammatical error in our story? B routine priorityPublic-health news

Arizona confirms first case of deadlier mpox strain - azcentral.com and The Arizona Republic

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first case of the more lethal Clade I mpox strain in a patient who acquired the infection while traveling internationally. This strain, known to cause more severe illness and higher death rates than the common Clade II strain, remains rare in the United States. The Arizona Department of Health Services emphasizes that the risk to the general public is low, with no evidence of local transmission. While the patient's details are withheld for privacy, federal health agencies note that as of June 2026, at least 20 Clade I cases have been reported nationwide, with cases increasing since March. Health officials recommend the same prevention measures for both strains, including vaccination, testing, and clinical management.
Original source text (English)
HEALTH Arizona confirms first case of deadlier mpox strain Stephanie Innes Arizona Republic July 23, 2026 Updated July 24, 2026, 9:43 a.m. MT Arizona health officials confirmed the state's first-ever case of a more lethal strain of mpox in a patient who acquired the infection while traveling outside of the United States. The Clade I strain of mpox is known to cause more serious illness and has higher death rates than the more common Clade II strain. Clade I is, to date, far more rare in the United States than Clade II mpox, which has been circulating in the country since 2022. The Arizona Department of Health Services said the risk to the general public is low and there's no evidence that Clade I mpox is spreading in Arizona. According to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, the risk posed by the Clade I mpox outbreak to most people remains low. The risk to men who have sex with men is low to moderate , the federal agency says. Federal health officials said that as of June 2026 there had been at least 20 Clade I mpox cases reported in the United States and that the incidence of the cases began to increase in frequency in the beginning of March 2026. Mpox, formerly known as monkeypox, is a viral infection similar to smallpox that typically causes painful rashes with sores and blisters and swollen lymph nodes and it typically spreads through skin-to-skin contact with the sores, blisters and body fluids. Other ways the virus can spread include: from infected animals; from sharing bedding, towels, clothing or other personal items; and prolonged close contact with someone who has mpox. The virus can pass to a baby during pregnancy, state health officials said, and casual contact in places such as stores, offices, or airplanes is unlikely to spread mpox. State is not providing details about the Arizona patient Citing patient privacy, the Arizona Department of Health Services would not disclose the age or gender of the person who was infected, nor would they disclose the county where the infected person resides, or if the person had been hospitalized. They also did not say whether the case was subclade Ia or Ib. The CDC as of June 2 was reporting that recent travel-associated Clade I mpox cases have almost exclusively been attributed to subclade Ib and that there have been no deaths associated with the cases. While the robust health care system in the United States has been able to treat people with Clade I mpox, globally it has proven more deadly, according to the CDC, with more than 55,000 confirmed cases since January 2024, including more than 150 deaths. As of July 21 there had been 34 cases of mpox reported to the state: 32 in Maricopa County, one in Cochise County and one in Yavapai County, state data shows. "We are monitoring them closely to better understand potential differences between Clade I and II," a news release from the Arizona Department of Health Services says. "Both types of mpox cause similar symptoms and we currently recommend using the same prevention measures including testing, vaccination, clinical management, and public health response." A global outbreak of the less severe Clade II strain of mpox began in 2022 and continues to this day, including in some African countries, according to the World Health Organization. Growing outbreaks of subclades Ia and Ib have also been affecting the Democratic Republic of the Congo and other countries in Africa, the WHO says. Subclade Ib was first detected beyond Africa in 2024, according to the WHO. Symptoms of mpox may include a new or unexplained rash that looks like pimples or blisters and may be painful or itchy. Some people may also experience fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, chills, exhaustion, or respiratory symptoms, state and federal health officials say, and people with weakened immune systems may be at greater risk for severe illness. Vaccine will help prevent mpox and reduce risk of severe illness Anyone who has had close physical contact with someone infected with mpox has an increased risk of contracting the disease and should get two doses of the JYNNEOS vaccine to both prevent infection and reduce the risk of severe illness. You can get vaccinated after an exposure. Vaccination is most effective when given within four days of exposure but may still reduce illness when given within 14 days, the state health department says. Check for symptoms for 21 days after your last contact. Anyone who develops symptoms should avoid close contact with others, isolate at home and contact a health provider for testing. Individuals who are infected with mpox should stay home and away from other people until all sores have healed, scabs have fallen off and a fresh layer of skin has formed. If you need to be around others, cover rashes or lesions with clothing, gloves or bandages, the state health department says. Other tips for avoiding the spread: Do not have sex or close physical contact if you or your partner has a new or unexplained rash, sores, or other symptoms of mpox. Condoms may reduce exposure in some areas of the body but may not prevent all mpox exposures because sores can appear elsewhere on the body. Consider reducing your number of partners. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Arizona reports first Clade I mpox case in resident with recent travel - upi.com

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first case of Clade I mpox, involving a resident with recent international travel. This marks the 34th mpox case in Arizona this year, with all previous cases being Clade II. The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) stated there is no evidence of local transmission connected to this case and is working to prevent additional infections through monitoring and follow-ups with potentially exposed individuals. Clade I, endemic to Central Africa, has been responsible for an ongoing global outbreak since late 2023, with over 55,000 confirmed cases and more than 150 deaths reported since January 2024. The U.S. has seen an increase in Clade I cases since March, with over 20 cases reported as of June.
Original source text (English)
U.S. News World News Korea Regional Review Latin America Region Odd News Entertainment Movies Music TV Sports NFL MLB NBA Soccer Voices Photos News US News World News Entertainment Sports Archive Almanac U.S. News July 23, 2026 / 1:28 AM Arizona reports first Clade I mpox case in resident with recent travel By Darryl Coote July 23 (UPI) -- Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first infection of an mpox strain responsible for a global outbreak, identifying the case as having been infected while traveling outside the United States. The Arizona Department of Health and Services said in a statement Wednesday that it has confirmed its first case of Clade I mpox, involving a person with recent international travel. "ADHS and local public health officials are working to prevent additional infections through monitoring and follow-ups with people who may have been exposed," the state health agency said. "There is currently no evidence of local transmission connected to this case." Little information about the patient was made public. The ADHS said it was notified of a confirmed travel-associated case of the mpox strain this month. It is the state's 34th case of the disease this year, with all but the most recent patient having contracted Clade II mpox. Mpox is an infection caused by the monkeypox virus, which is spread through physical contact, including direct skin-to-skin contact and sharing of personal items, including bedding, towels and clothing, the ADHS said. The virus has two main clades: Clade I, which is endemic to Central Africa, and Clade II, which is associated with West Africa. According to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, Clade I was historically reported to have a much higher case-fatality rate than Clade II, though recent data suggest case-fatality rates for both clades are low, especially when medical care is provided. Clade II is the cause of the ongoing mpox outbreak that began in 2022, infecting more than 114,000 people worldwide, including about 37,500 people infected in the United States, CDC statistics show. ADHS statistics show Arizona had 582 mpox infections in 2022, followed by 20 in 2023, 66 in 2024 and 17 last year. Clade I is also responsible for an ongoing mpox outbreak that began in Central Africa in late 2023, and which has since spread internationally, resulting in more than 55,000 confirmed cases, including more than 150 deaths, since January 2024, the CDC said. The United States reported between 1,700 and 2,800 mpox cases a year from 2023 through 2025, though nearly all have been Clade II. The CDC said Clade I cases have increased in frequency in the country since March, with more than 20 cases reported in the United States as of June. B routine priorityPublic-health news

ADHS Confirms Arizona’s First Case of Clade I Mpox; - The Gila Herald

News Brief
The Arizona Department of Health Services has confirmed the state's first travel-associated case of Clade I mpox. The individual acquired the infection during travel, and while the case is confirmed, officials emphasize that the risk to the general public remains low. This marks a significant public health development as Clade I mpox is a more virulent strain than the previously predominant Clade II. Health authorities are expected to monitor the situation closely, though no further details on the patient's condition or potential contacts have been released at this time.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Department of Health confirms first case of Clade I mpox in Arizona but says public risk low - KTAR News 92.3 FM

News Brief
The Arizona Department of Health has confirmed the state's first case of Clade I mpox, emphasizing that the public risk remains low. This marks a significant development in the region's disease monitoring, though no further details on transmission, exposures, or health impacts have been disclosed. Authorities are likely conducting follow-up investigations to contain potential spread, though the low-risk assessment suggests limited community transmission at this time. The public is urged to remain vigilant but not alarmed, as health officials continue to monitor the situation closely.

贝努埃调查疑似猴痘案件,敦促居民保持冷静 - 真实广播网

Benue Probes Suspected Mpox Case, Urges Residents to Remain Calm - Real Broadcasting Network
新闻简报尼日利亚贝努埃州正在调查科西沙地方政府地区的一起疑似 猴痘 案件,此前科林亚一处住所传出报告。州卫生专员 Paul Ogwuche 博士证实了这一调查,但强调尚未确认 暴发。当局已派遣公共卫生官员评估情况并收集样本进行实验室检测。该州的活动性疾病 监测 系统正在与国内和国际卫生合作伙伴合作,以确保早期发现。建议居民保持冷静,避免传播未经证实的信息,如果出现发烧并伴有不明原因的皮疹或水泡,请寻求医疗评估。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-26。活动日期:周六(具体日期未公布)。
地点与对象
尼日利亚贝努埃州,特别是 Konshisha 地方政府区、Korinya、Tyokula Achihi 住宅区。
事件
贝努埃州政府正在调查一处住宅报告的疑似 猴痘 病例。卫生与公众服务专员 Paul Ogwuche 博士证实了调查结果,但表示尚未确认 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
通报1例疑似病例。无确诊病例、死亡病例或 暴发。风险级别没有明确说明,但当局强调没有确认 暴发。
应对、状态与不确定性
公共卫生总监和州流行病学家已部署疾病 监测 和通报官员进行实地评估、病例调查和实验室检测样本收集。该州的疾病 监测 系统在所有 23 个地方政府行政区中均处于活跃状态。与国家疾病预防控制中心、联邦卫生部、世界卫生组织和发展伙伴的合作正在进行中。敦促居民保持冷静并向卫生机构报告症状。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Benue Probes Suspected Mpox Case, Urges Residents to Remain Calm - Real Broadcasting Network

News Brief
Benue State, Nigeria, is investigating a suspected Mpox case in Konshisha Local Government Area after reports emerged from a residence in Korinya. State Health Commissioner Dr Paul Ogwuche confirmed the investigation but emphasized no outbreak has been confirmed. Authorities have deployed public health officials to assess the situation and collect samples for laboratory testing. The state's active disease surveillance system is collaborating with national and international health partners to ensure early detection. Residents are advised to remain calm, avoid spreading unverified information, and seek medical evaluation if experiencing fever with unexplained rashes or blisters.
Original source text (English)
Benue Probes Suspected Mpox Case, Urges Residents to Remain Calm By Damilola Olufemi 1 min read File image: A man with Mpox The Benue State Government has commenced an investigation into a suspected case of Mpox reported in Konshisha Local Government Area, saying no outbreak has been confirmed in the state. The state Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr Paul Ogwuche, disclosed this on Saturday while responding to reports of a suspected case at the Tyokula Achihi residence in Korinya, Konshisha Local Government Area. According to him, the Director of Public Health was directed to liaise with the State Epidemiologist, who in turn immediately deployed the Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha Local Government Area to the reported location for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation and collection of appropriate clinical samples for laboratory testing. “At this stage, no Mpox outbreak has been confirmed in Benue State. It is important to emphasise that skin rashes and fever can result from several medical conditions, and laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made,” he said. The commissioner noted that the state’s disease surveillance system remains active across all 23 local government areas. According to him, the ministry is collaborating with the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), the Federal Ministry of Health, the World Health Organisation (WHO) and other development partners to ensure early detection and prompt response to public health threats. He called on residents to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information that could cause unnecessary panic. “We encourage anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes or blisters to report immediately to the nearest health facility for proper medical evaluation and to avoid close physical contact until assessed by healthcare professionals,” he said. What is Mpox? Mpox is an infectious disease caused by the monkeypox virus. It spreads through close skin-to-skin contact, sexual activity, or touching contaminated items like bedding and towels. Symptoms include a painful rash, swollen lymph nodes, and fever. Most people recover fully within 2 to 4 weeks

Scale, impact and risk
One suspected case reported. No confirmed cases, deaths, or outbreak. Risk level not explicitly stated, but authorities emphasize no outbreak confirmed.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Director of Public Health and State Epidemiologist have deployed a Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation, and sample collection for laboratory testing. The state's disease surveillance system is active across all 23 LGAs. Collaboration with NCDC, Federal Ministry of Health, WHO, and development partners is ongoing. Residents are urged to remain calm and report symptoms to health facilities.

FDA 授权首次针对 HSV、VZV 和 猴痘 皮肤病变进行多重 PCR 检测 - Contagion Live

FDA Authorizes First Multiplex PCR Assay for HSV, VZV, and Mpox Skin Lesions - Contagion Live
新闻简报FDA 已授权首个多重 PCR 检测方法,用于同时检测皮肤病变中的 HSV-1、HSV-2、VZV 和 猴痘病毒,从而解决了关键的诊断空白。Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV 检测(Seegene USA)获得了紧急使用授权,使临床医生能够在单次测试中区分这些病毒。考虑到这些病原体的临床表现与美国正在进行的 猴痘 传播 重叠,这一点尤其重要。2024 年的一项研究通过在 5.6% 的单靶点方法阴性样本中检测到其中一种病毒,证明了该检测的价值。虽然该授权简化了测试并可能加速 治疗 决策,但敏感性和特异性数据并未披露,要求临床医生在实施前审查完整的 EUA 文件。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026-07-22。证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
受影响人群:有水疱、脓疱或溃疡性皮肤损害的患者,特别是疑似猴痘、生殖器疱疹或带状疱疹的患者。
事件
FDA 授予 Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV 检测(Seegene USA)紧急使用授权 (EUA),这是第一个多重 PCR 检测,旨在在一次运行中同时区分 HSV-1、HSV-2、VZV 和 猴痘病毒 与皮肤病变。这是已确认的监管授权。
规模、影响与风险
没有病例数或死亡报告。该检测解决了诊断空白,因为 2024 年的一项研究发现,它在 966 个样本中的 54 个样本中检测到了 HSV、VZV 或 MPXV,此前单靶点方法呈阴性(检出率 5.6%)。未明确说明风险级别。
应对、状态与不确定性
EUA 将该检测定位为针对未分化皮肤病变的解决方案。新闻稿中未披露敏感性和特异性数据。建议临床医生在实施前查看 FDA EUA 摘要和检测说明以供使用。该授权与美国正在进行的 猴痘 传播 相关,MPXV 分支 IIb 自 2022 年以来一直在传播,而分支 I 则记录在旅行者和散发病例中。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

FDA Authorizes First Multiplex PCR Assay for HSV, VZV, and Mpox Skin Lesions - Contagion Live

News Brief
The FDA has authorized the first multiplex PCR assay for simultaneous detection of HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and mpox virus from skin lesions, addressing a critical diagnostic gap. The Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay (Seegene USA) received Emergency Use Authorization, enabling clinicians to differentiate these viruses in a single test run. This is particularly important given the overlapping clinical presentations of these pathogens and the ongoing mpox transmission in the US. A 2024 study demonstrated the assay's value by detecting one of these viruses in 5.6% of samples previously negative by single-target methods. While the authorization streamlines testing and may accelerate treatment decisions, sensitivity and specificity data were not disclosed, requiring clinicians to review the full EUA documentation before implementation.
Original source text (English)
The FDA has granted EUA for the Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay (Seegene USA), the first multiplex PCR test designed to simultaneously differentiate HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and mpox virus from ulcerative skin lesions in a single run. According to a new announcement from Seegene, Inc, the FDA has granted Emergency Use Authorization (EUA) for its Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay, described as the first multiplex PCR test designed to support differential diagnosis of viral causes of ulcerative skin lesions in the US market, with the ability to simultaneously detect and differentiate herpes simplex virus type 1 (HSV-1), herpes simplex virus type 2 (HSV-2), varicella-zoster virus (VZV), and mpox virus (MPXV clades I/II and clade II) from a single clinical sample. 1 HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and MPXV all produce vesicular, pustular, or ulcerative skin lesions with overlapping clinical presentations, making visual differentiation unreliable. Single-pathogen molecular testing requires separate orders and samples for each suspected pathogen, creating a diagnostic gap when the causative virus is not among those targeted. The EUA positions the Allplex assay as a solution for clinicians encountering undifferentiated skin lesion presentations in settings where mpox, genital herpes, and herpes zoster may be in the differential. “Seegene’s high-multiplex PCR technologies have been widely utilized across global markets,” Jun Kim, CEO of Seegene USA, said in a statement. 1 “This authorization marks an important step in bringing those capabilities more fully into the US market through Seegene USA, enabling the development of locally relevant multiplex diagnostic products.” The Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay was developed end-to-end at Seegene USA using three proprietary high-multiplex PCR technologies: Dual Priming Oligonucleotide (DPO), which improves specificity in complex multiplex reactions; Tagging Oligonucleotide Cleavage and Extension (TOCE), which enables simultaneous detection of multiple targets at distinct temperature channels; and Multiple Detection Temperatures (MuDT), which allows individual analyte identification within a single fluorescence channel. 1 The assay was built using Seegene's automated Seegene Digitalized Development System platform, designed to maintain sensitivity and specificity across high-multiplex configurations. What is the Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay authorized for? The Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay (Seegene USA) received FDA Emergency Use Authorization for simultaneous detection and differentiation of HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and mpox virus (MPXV clades I/II and clade II) from vesicular, pustular, or ulcerative skin lesion samples in a single PCR reaction. Why is multiplex testing for skin lesion pathogens clinically important? HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and mpox virus all cause overlapping vesicular or ulcerative skin presentations. Single-pathogen testing misses cases where the causative virus is not among those ordered; a poster at the 2024 Clinical Virology Symposium found the multiplex approach detected HSV, VZV, or MPXV in 54 of 966 samples previously negative by single-target methods. No. The Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay received an Emergency Use Authorization, not traditional FDA approval. Clinicians and laboratory directors should review the FDA EUA summary and the assay’s instructions for use for full analytical performance data before clinical implementation. In a poster presented at the 2024 Clinical Virology Symposium, researchers evaluated 966 samples previously reported as negative by single-pathogen molecular testing methods. 1 The multiplex PCR approach identified HSV, VZV, or MPXV clade I/II in 54 of those 966 samples, a 5.6% rate of detection in specimens considered negative by the existing testing paradigm, underscoring the diagnostic gap created by single-target testing. The authorization is clinically relevant against the backdrop of ongoing mpox transmission in the United States . MPXV clade IIb has circulated in the US since the 2022 global outbreak, and clade I has been documented in travelers and sporadic domestic cases in subsequent years. 2 The clinical overlap between mpox lesions and HSV or VZV reactivation is a documented source of misdiagnosis, particularly in HIV-positive patients and other immunocompromised individuals where all three infections carry significant morbidity. For infection preventionists and ID clinicians managing patients with ulcerative genital or cutaneous lesions, the assay's ability to simultaneously rule in or rule out HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and MPXV in a single run addresses a workflow inefficiency with direct clinical and public health consequences. 1 A patient presenting with a vesiculopustular rash currently requires sequential testing with separate collection swabs and test orders if the treating clinician suspects more than one viral etiology. Single-tube multiplex testing compresses process and may accelerate isolation decisions, antiviral initiation, and contact notification. Sensitivity and specificity data from the EUA validation studies were not disclosed in the press release. Clinicians and laboratory directors should consult the FDA EUA summary and the Allplex assay’s instructions for use for analytical performance characteristics before clinical implementation. Seegene stated the EUA also advances the company’s Technology Sharing Initiative, a program supporting regional partners in developing and manufacturing multiplex molecular tests tailored to local diagnostic needs globally. 1 Solange Peters, MD, PhD; Benjamin P. Time
Published on 2026-07-22. Event date not specified in the evidence.
Location and population
Affected population: patients with vesicular, pustular, or ulcerative skin lesions, particularly those with suspected mpox, genital herpes, or herpes zoster.
Event
The FDA granted Emergency Use Authorization (EUA) for the Allplex HSV-1&2/VZV/MPXV Assay (Seegene USA), the first multiplex PCR test designed to simultaneously differentiate HSV-1, HSV-2, VZV, and mpox virus from skin lesions in a single run. This is a confirmed regulatory authorization.
Scale, impact and risk
No case counts or deaths reported. The assay addresses a diagnostic gap, as a 2024 study found it detected HSV, VZV, or MPXV in 54 of 966 samples previously negative by single-target methods (5.6% detection rate). Risk level not explicitly stated.
Response, status and uncertainty
The EUA positions the assay as a solution for undifferentiated skin lesions. Sensitivity and specificity data were not disclosed in the press release. Clinicians are advised to review the FDA EUA summary and assay instructions for use before implementation. The authorization is relevant amid ongoing mpox transmission in the US, with MPXV clade IIb circulating since 2022 and clade I documented in travelers and sporadic cases.

旅行者给亚利桑那州带来了更严重的 猴痘 压力,该州加大了对暴露者的搜寻力度

Traveler Brings Harsher Mpox Strain to Arizona, State Ramps Up Hunt for Exposures
新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认一名在国外感染的旅行者感染了 I 型 猴痘 菌株,这是一种更严重的菌株。诊断 于 2026 年 7 月 22 日制作,没有本地 传播 的证据。这使得该州今年的 猴痘 案件总数达到 34 起。历史上,I 型分支的病死率较高,为 1-10%。卫生当局正在进行接触者追踪,提供暴露后 疫苗接种,并敦促加强 监测。疫苗接种 建议用于高危人群和可能出现症状的人群,JYNNEOS 疫苗 可有效对抗多个分支。总体公共风险仍然较低。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月23日。活动日期:诊断于2026年7月22日确认。
地点与对象
受影响人群:在国外旅行时感染的旅行者。没有本地 传播 的证据。
事件
亚利桑那州卫生官员证实,一名居民的 I 型 猴痘 基因检测呈阳性,这是一种与更严重疾病相关的基因菌株。此案与国际旅行有关。
规模、影响与风险
截至 7 月 21 日,亚利桑那州今年已记录了 34 例 猴痘 病例。I 型分支历史上的病死率在 1% 至 10% 之间。公众面临的总体风险仍然很低。
应对、状态与不确定性
州政府团队正在追踪接触者,并与临床医生合作,以确定接触者并提供检测或 疫苗接种。触发增强的 监测。建议高风险人群使用 疫苗接种,并对有症状的个体进行及时检测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Traveler Brings Harsher Mpox Strain to Arizona, State Ramps Up Hunt for Exposures

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed a case of clade I mpox, a more severe strain, in a traveler who acquired the infection abroad. The diagnosis was made on July 22, 2026, with no evidence of local transmission. This brings the state's total mpox cases to 34 this year. Clade I has historically been associated with a higher case fatality ratio of 1-10%. Health authorities are conducting contact tracing, offering post-exposure vaccination, and urging heightened surveillance. Vaccination is recommended for high-risk individuals and those with possible symptoms, with JYNNEOS vaccine effective against multiple clades. The overall public risk remains low.
Original source text (English)

Tech & MedicineParks & NatureGet the latest local stories in your inbox.SubscribeMore forecasts: Atlanta 30 day forecastPhoenixTraveler Brings Harsher Mpox Strain to Arizona, State Ramps Up Hunt for ExposuresBy Taylor JohnsonPublished on July 23, 2026Source:US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention Arizona health officials say a traveler has brought a more worrisome form of mpox into the state, confirming Wednesday that a resident tested positive for clade I, a genetic strain public health experts have linked to more severe illness. The case appears tied to international travel, and the Arizona Department of Health Services reports there is currently no sign the virus is spreading locally. State teams are tracing contacts and working with clinicians to identify anyone who may have been exposed and to offer testing or vaccination when appropriate. In a statement to FOX 10 Phoenix, the department said the diagnosis was confirmed on July 22 and that the patient "acquired the infection while traveling outside the country." Officials told the outlet that investigators have not found evidence of local transmission linked to this case and that they are following standard contact tracing protocols in coordination with local health jurisdictions. Clade I, formerly known as the Congo Basin clade, has historically been associated with more severe disease than clade II, with reported case fatality ratios in some series roughly between 1% and 10%, according to the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health. That analysis also notes that vaccines and treatments used in the United States, including the two dose JYNNEOS vaccine, are expected to provide protection against multiple clades, although clinicians are encouraged to test for and document clade information whenever possible. What health officials recommend Health officials advise that anyone who might have been exposed to mpox contact a healthcare provider or local health department so they can be evaluated for testing and post exposure vaccination. The CDC recommends that JYNNEOS be given as soon as possible after exposure, ideally within four days for the best chance to prevent illness, and notes that vaccination up to 14 days after exposure may still help reduce how severe the disease becomes. Local context and next steps The Arizona Department of Health Services told FOX 10 Phoenix that the state has logged 34 mpox cases so far this year as of July 21. Officials said the newly confirmed clade I infection will trigger heightened surveillance, even as they stress that the overall risk to the general public remains low. Public health leaders continue to urge vaccination for people at higher risk and prompt testing for anyone who develops symptoms. People who recently traveled to a country with ongoing clade I transmission, or who develop possible mpox symptoms such as fever, swollen lymph nodes, rectal pain or a blistering rash, are urged to contact a healthcare provider and avoid close physical contact until they have been evaluated. State and federal guidance on testing, isolation and vaccine eligibility is available from the CDC.Phoenix-Health & LifestyleHealth & Lifestyle in ...All CitiesPhoenixShow CommentsPlease enable JavaScript to view the comments powered by Disqus.Trending in PhoenixAbout 4 hours agoFeds Put Six Phoenix Hospitals on Blast in Price Transparency CrackdownAbout 4 hours agoPhoenix Man Walks Out Of Jail, Into Deadly Street HeatAbout 4 hours agoPhoenix Family Won't Let 2006 Killing Go, Puts Up $7K RewardAbout 5 hours agoDust Wall Turns I-10 Near Picacho Peak Into Blind ZoneLoad MoreNationalBay Area's Oliver Tree Killed When Two Helicopters Smashed Into Each Other Above Rio de JaneiroSan Francisco Appeals Court Says Hiring a Hit Man Is "Not Categorically a Crime of Violence"Beach-Blocking Billionaire Ditches the Niners to Buy the Team That Ruined Their SeasonFloyd Mayweather Charged With Felony Theft Over $200K Watch Bought With a Bounced CheckOakland Firefighters Gave a Smoke-Choked Pigeon Oxygen — and 11 Million People Lost ItFounder of Real SF Startup Is Cutting Up Banned Target Bags and Calling Them Dog Raincoats. First Clade I mpox case confirmed in Arizona, risk to public remains low
新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认该州首例与旅行相关的 I 型 猴痘 病例,患者为一名在国外感染该病毒的居民。亚利桑那州卫生服务部 (ADHS) 于 7 月收到通知,强调公众面临的风险很小。本地 传播 尚未与此案关联。截至 7 月 21 日,亚利桑那州已记录了 34 例 猴痘 病例,同时包含 Clade I 和 II 变体。卫生官员正在监测暴露的个人,并推荐 疫苗接种、测试和隔离方案。I 型分支病例在美国并不常见,通常发生在非洲地区。这两个分支都产生类似的症状,当局建议不要与有症状的个体密切接触。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

First Clade I mpox case confirmed in Arizona, risk to public remains low

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first travel-related Clade I mpox case in a resident who contracted the virus abroad. The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) received notification in July and emphasizes that the general public faces minimal risk. No local transmission has been linked to this case. As of July 21, Arizona has documented 34 mpox cases combining both Clade I and II variants. Health officials are monitoring exposed individuals and recommend vaccination, testing, and isolation protocols. Clade I cases are uncommon in the United States, typically occurring in African regions. Both clades produce similar symptoms, and authorities advise against close contact with symptomatic individuals.
Original source text (English)

No evidence suggests local transmission linked to this case, according to ADHS.Two distinct types of mpox exist: Clade I and Clade II. Arizona has documented Clade II mpox cases spreading throughout the state and nation since 2022.ADHS issued alerts to healthcare providers regarding rising mpox cases reported this summer. Through July 21, 2026, Arizona documented 34 mpox cases combining both Clade I and II variants.Clade I cases typically occur in African regions and remain uncommon in the United States. Officials are closely monitoring these cases to identify potential differences between the two clades.Both mpox types produce similar symptoms, and health authorities currently recommend identical prevention measures. These include testing, vaccination, clinical management, and public health response protocols.The monkeypox virus causes mpox infection, primarily spreading through close personal contact with infected individuals, according to ADHS. Transmission occurs through direct skin-to-skin contact with rashes, sores, scabs or body fluids, as well as through close physical contact.Sharing bedding, towels, clothing, or personal items can spread the virus. Prolonged close contact with infected individuals poses transmission risk, and pregnant individuals can pass the infection to their babies.Casual encounters in stores, offices or airplanes are unlikely to spread mpox, according to health officials.Symptoms include new or unexplained rashes resembling pimples or blisters that may cause pain or itching. Additional symptoms can include fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, chills, exhaustion, or respiratory issues.Individuals with compromised immune systems face greater risk for severe illness. The JYNNEOS vaccine can prevent mpox and reduce severe illness risk, with two doses providing optimal protection, ADHS said.People recently exposed to infected individuals should contact healthcare providers or local health departments immediately. Post-exposure vaccination works most effectively within four days but may still reduce illness severity when administered within 14 days.Exposed individuals should monitor for symptoms for 21 days following their last contact. Anyone developing symptoms should avoid close contact with others, isolate at home and contact healthcare providers for testing.Infected individuals must remain home and away from others until all sores heal, scabs fall off and fresh skin forms. Rashes or lesions should be covered with clothing, gloves or bandages if proximity to others becomes necessary.Health officials advise against skin-to-skin contact and sharing bedding, towels, clothing, or personal items with symptomatic or diagnosed individuals. Sexual transmission remains possible, and individuals should avoid sex or close physical contact if either partner shows new rashes, sores or other mpox symptoms.Condoms may reduce exposure in some body areas but cannot prevent all mpox exposures since sores can appear anywhere on the body, ADHS said. Reducing partner numbers may lower risk.ADHS will continue monitoring mpox activity and providing updates to healthcare providers and public health partners as new information emerges. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

猴痘:贝努埃调查Konshisha LG的疑似病例

Mpox: Benue investigates suspected cases in Konshisha LG
新闻简报尼日利亚贝努埃州正在调查科林亚 (Korinya) 地区的 猴痘 疑似病例,此前居民报告了几名年轻人出现严重皮疹、发烧和水泡。贝努埃州卫生部启动了疾病监测系统,并派出人员评估和收集样本,但尚未确认暴发。受影响的个人无法获得适当的 诊断 和 治疗,引发了对潜在传播的担忧。社区已呼吁国际和国家卫生机构进行紧急干预。当局敦促大家保持冷静,并强调需要进行实验室检测才能确认,预计很快就会有结果。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布日期:2026年7月25日。事件日期:未明确说明,但在2026年7月25日报告疑似病例后启动调查。
地点与对象
地区:贝努埃州。社区:Korinya、Konshisha 当地政府区 (LGA)。受影响人群:位于 Korinya Vandeikya 路 Km 2 沿线的 Tyokula Achihi Residence 的几名年轻男女。
事件
发生了什么:贝努埃州卫生与公众服务部在居民拨打求救电话后对可疑的 猴痘 暴发 展开调查。举报人:Korinya社区居民。状态:怀疑,未确认。贝努埃州尚未确认 猴痘 暴发。
规模、影响与风险
病例:几名年轻男女出现严重皮疹、发烧和水疱样病变。死亡人数:未报告。暴露:疾病在近距离居住的人群中迅速传播,同一院落中许多人受到感染。风险:居民担心,如果不加以遏制,病毒可能会蔓延到更广泛的科林亚社区及其他地区。卫生系统影响:受影响的人无法获得适当的 诊断、隔离或 治疗。
应对、状态与不确定性
调查:疾病监测系统被激活。公共卫生主任与国家流行病学家合作。疾病 监测 和通知官员 (DSNO) 部署到受影响的社区进行评估和样本收集。检测:需实验室检测确认;尚未确认 暴发。预防:敦促公众报告症状并避免密切接触。沟通:当实验室结果出来时,调查结果将被公开。不确定性:需要实验室确认;皮疹和发烧可能是由多种疾病引起的。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Mpox: Benue investigates suspected cases in Konshisha LG

News Brief
Benue State, Nigeria, is investigating suspected Mpox cases in Korinya, Konshisha LGA, after residents reported several young people with severe rashes, fever, and blisters. The Benue State Ministry of Health activated its disease surveillance system and deployed officers to assess and collect samples, but no outbreak has been confirmed. Affected individuals lack access to proper diagnosis and treatment, raising fears of potential spread. The community has appealed to international and national health agencies for urgent intervention. Authorities urge calm and emphasize that laboratory testing is necessary for confirmation, with results expected soon.
Original source text (English)
Vanguard News Home News Top Stories E-editions Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns ALLURE Search for: Home News Top Stories E-editions Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns ALLURE Home News E-Editions Politics Metro Business Sports Editorial Columns Allure Home » News » Mpox: Benue investigates suspected cases in Konshisha LG News Follow us on Google July 25, 2026 Mpox: Benue investigates suspected cases in Konshisha LG Layer 1 By Peter Duru The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services has launched an investigation into reports of a suspected Mpox outbreak in Korinya, Konshisha Local Government Area, LGA, following a distress call by residents who appealed for urgent government and international intervention over an unidentified skin disease affecting several people in their community. The alarm was raised by residents of Tyokula Achihi Residence, located along Km 2 Vandeikya Road, Korinya, in a public appeal dated July 25, 2026. The residents claimed that several young men and women had developed severe rashes, fever and blister-like lesions covering their faces, chests, backs and other parts of their bodies, raising fears of a possible Mpox outbreak. In the statement signed by Shadrach Mtsor on behalf of the community, the residents said the illness appeared to be spreading rapidly among people living in close quarters, with many of the affected individuals reportedly being residents within the same compound. “We are making a desperate public appeal for immediate medical intervention over a suspected outbreak of M-Pox in our community. Many of the affected persons are staff and residents within the same compound and have no access to proper diagnosis, isolation or treatment. We fear this could spread to the wider Korinya community and beyond if not contained now,” the statement read. The community appealed to the World Health Organization (WHO), the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), the Federal Ministry of Health, the Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services, the Benue State Emergency Management Agency (BSEMA), UNICEF, the Nigerian Red Cross and Médecins Sans Frontières (MSF) to urgently deploy medical personnel to investigate the cases. Among the interventions requested were the immediate deployment of a rapid response medical team to assess and test the suspected cases, provision of isolation materials, personal protective equipment and hygiene kits, free medical treatment for affected persons, public health education to prevent further transmission, and possible temporary closure and decontamination of the affected workplace if necessary. “Our community is poor and cannot handle this alone. The victims are isolated in their rooms without medical care. We are scared. We appeal to all well-meaning bodies, NGOs and government agencies to come to our aid before this becomes a bigger epidemic,” the residents added. Responding to the reports, the Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr. Paul Ogwuche, said the Ministry had immediately activated its disease surveillance system in line with established public health protocols after becoming aware of the reports circulating on social media. According to the Commissioner, the Director of Public Health was instructed to work with the State Epidemiologist, who promptly deployed the Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha LGA to the affected community to conduct an on-the-ground assessment, investigate the suspected cases and collect clinical samples for laboratory analysis. Dr. Ogwuche, however, stressed that no Mpox outbreak had been confirmed in Benue State, noting that laboratory testing remained the only basis for confirming the disease.Related NewsFresh attacks rock Benue's Sankera axis; one killedLassa fever death toll hits 229 as fresh infections riseLassa fever death toll hits 229 as fresh infections rise “At this stage, no Mpox outbreak has been confirmed in Benue State. It is important to emphasise that skin rashes and fever can result from several medical conditions, and laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made,” he stated. The commissioner assured residents that the administration of Governor Hyacinth Alia remained committed to protecting public health, adding that disease surveillance remained active across all 23 LGA of the state in collaboration with the NCDC, the Federal Ministry of Health, the WHO and other development partners. He urged members of the public to remain calm and refrain from spreading unverified information capable of causing unnecessary panic, while encouraging anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes or blisters to report immediately to the nearest health facility for proper medical evaluation and to avoid close physical contact until assessed by healthcare professionals. Dr. Ogwuche also commended members of the Korinya community for promptly reporting the suspected cases, describing early reporting as critical to effective disease surveillance and emergency response. He assured that the Ministry would make the findings of the investigation public as soon as laboratory results become available, adding that appropriate response measures would be implemented immediately in accordance with national public health guidelines if the suspected cases were confirmed to be Mpox or any other disease of public health concern. Benue State, Nigeria, is investigating a suspected Mpox case reported in Konshisha Local Government Area. 卫生部确认亚利桑那州首例 Clade I 猴痘 病例,但表示公共风险较低

Department of Health confirms first case of Clade I mpox in Arizona but says public risk low
新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认该州首例 I 型 猴痘 病例,由一名居民在国际旅行期间感染。虽然公共风险被认为较低,但这使得今年亚利桑那州的 猴痘 病例总数达到 34 例,其中包括 Clade I 和 Clade II 变种。亚利桑那州卫生服务部正在积极监测情况,对接触者进行跟踪,并强调没有当地的 传播 与此病例有关。卫生当局建议采取预防措施,包括针对高危人群使用 疫苗接种 和 JYNNEOS 疫苗,并建议对感染者进行隔离,直至症状完全缓解。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 22 日;活动已于周三(2026 年 7 月 22 日)确认。
地点与对象
美国亚利桑那州;一名在出国旅行时感染病毒的居民。
事件
亚利桑那州卫生服务部 (ADHS) 确认了亚利桑那州首例 Clade I 猴痘 病例。该病例与旅行有关,没有迹象表明当地是传播。
规模、影响与风险
截至周二,今年亚利桑那州已报告 34 例 猴痘 病例(包括 Clade I 和 Clade II)。ADHS 表示公共风险较低。
应对、状态与不确定性
ADHS 正在监测病例,以了解 Clade I 和 Clade II 之间的区别。正在对可能暴露的个人进行后续行动。未检测到本地 传播。该部门建议高危人群使用 JYNNEOS 疫苗,并建议对感染者进行隔离。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Department of Health confirms first case of Clade I mpox in Arizona but says public risk low

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first case of Clade I mpox, contracted by a resident during international travel. While the public risk is deemed low, this brings the total mpox cases in Arizona to 34 this year, including both Clade I and Clade II variants. The Arizona Department of Health Services is actively monitoring the situation, conducting follow-ups with exposed individuals, and emphasizing that no local transmission has been linked to this case. Health authorities recommend preventive measures, including vaccination with the JYNNEOS vaccine for those at risk, and advise isolation for infected individuals until symptoms fully resolve.
Original source text (English)

BY KYLIE WERNER Jul 22, 2026, 8:59 PM 1 Follow on Google News Watch: BREAKING: U.S. Sen. Ruben Gallego says he’s considering running for president on KTAR | 22 mins The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) confirmed the first case of Clade I mpox in Arizona on Wednesday. There are two types of mpox, Clade I and Clade II, and as of Tuesday 34 cases have been reported in the state between the two this year. In fact, there have been cases of Clade II mpox spreading in Arizona and the U.S. since 2022. The department is monitoring them to try and understand the difference between the two types as they have similar symptoms and the same prevention measures are recommended for both. ADVERTISEMENT ADHS was notified this month that a resident contracted the infection while traveling outside the country. The department and public health officials are doing their part to prevent spread of the virus by doing follow-ups with people potentially exposed. It noted that there is currently no indication of local transmission connected to the case. ADHS said that Clade I cases are typically found in parts of Africa and are less common in the U.S. What are the symptoms of Mpox? Mpox is caused by the monkeypox virus and spread through close contact with someone who is infected. That includes skin-to-skin contact with rash, sores, scabs or bodily fluids as well as sharing bedding, towels, clothing or other items. It was also noted that mpox can be passed during pregnancy to a baby. ADHS highlighted that the infection is unlikely to spread through casual contact at stores, offices or on airplanes. One of the symptoms of mpox is a rash that looks like pimples or blisters, which can be painful or itchy. There are some who also get a fever, headache, muscles aches, swollen lymph nodes, chills, exhaustion or respiratory symptoms. It was advised that people who believe they are at risk get the JYNNEOS vaccine, which requires two doses for the best protection. The vaccine is most effective within four days of exposure, but might still reduce the infection when received within 14 days. Those who are exposed should contact a healthcare provider or local health department, get tested and check for symptoms for 21 days after the contact. People who develop symptoms are also advised to isolate at home and avoid close contact with others. Those who test positive for mpox are told to isolate at home and stay away from people until their sores have healed, scabs have fallen off and there is a fresh layer of skin. People who are infected that have to be around others should cover their rashes or lesions with clothing, gloves or bandages. View five news elements
Time
Published on July 22, 2026; event confirmed on Wednesday (July 22, 2026).
Location and population
Arizona, USA; a resident who contracted the infection while traveling outside the country.
Event
The Arizona Department of Health Services (ADHS) confirmed the first case of Clade I mpox in Arizona. The case is travel-related, and there is no indication of local transmission.
Scale, impact and risk
As of Tuesday, 34 mpox cases (both Clade I and Clade II) have been reported in Arizona this year. ADHS states the public risk is low.
Response, status and uncertainty
ADHS is monitoring cases to understand the difference between Clade I and Clade II. Follow-ups are being conducted with potentially exposed individuals. No local transmission has been detected. The department recommends the JYNNEOS vaccine for at-risk individuals and advises isolation for those infected.

Health Ministry Confirms Third Mpox Case in Costa Rica: Patient Resides in Heredia - The Costa Rica News

News Brief
Costa Rica's Health Ministry has confirmed the third mpox case in the country, with the patient residing in Heredia. This brings the total number of confirmed cases to three. No additional details about the patient's condition, potential exposures, or public health measures have been released. The announcement comes as health authorities continue to monitor the situation, though the current risk level remains unspecified.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Govt dispels rumours of Mpox outbreak in Benue - The Sun Nigeria

News Brief
The Nigerian government has officially refuted rumors of an mpox outbreak in Benue state, as reported on July 26, 2026. The clarification comes amid unverified claims circulating about the disease's presence in the region. Authorities have not confirmed any cases, deaths, or exposures related to mpox in Benue. This official statement aims to prevent public panic and misinformation regarding the disease's spread in the area. The government's swift response underscores the importance of accurate information during health-related rumors.
Original source text (English)

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

亚利桑那州卫生服务部确认亚利桑那州首例与旅行相关的 Clade I 猴痘 病例;公共风险仍然较低

Arizona Department of Health Services confirms Arizona's first travel-associated Clade I Mpox case; public risk remains low
新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认该州首例与旅行相关的 Clade I 猴痘 病例,该菌株在非洲部分地区更为常见。受感染的居民在国际旅行时感染了该病毒,没有证据表明亚利桑那州当地存在 传播。亚利桑那州卫生服务部表示,公众面临的总体风险仍然很低。这使得该州 2026 年 猴痘 病例总数达到 34 例,其中包括 I 型和 II 型感染。卫生官员正在对接触者进行追踪并监测接触者,以防止进一步传播。自 2022 年以来,分支 II 一直在美国流行,而分支 I 病例仍然不常见。建议高风险人群使用 疫苗接种,任何有症状的人都应隔离并寻求检测。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-22;证据中未指定事件日期。
地点与对象
亚利桑那州,美国;在美国境外旅行时感染病毒的受感染居民
事件
亚利桑那州卫生服务部确认该州首例与旅行相关的 I 型 猴痘 病例;没有本地 传播 的证据;对公众的风险仍然很低。
规模、影响与风险
亚利桑那州在 2026 年报告了 34 例 猴痘 病例(包括 I 型和 II 型);I 型分支病例在美国仍然不常见;没有提及死亡或严重后果;风险明确表述为低。
应对、状态与不确定性
州和地方卫生官员进行接触者追踪和监测接触者;疫苗接种推荐给高危人群;全州范围内持续监测;没有确认本地 传播。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Arizona Department of Health Services confirms Arizona's first travel-associated Clade I Mpox case; public risk remains low

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first travel-related case of Clade I mpox, a strain more common in parts of Africa. The infected resident contracted the virus while traveling internationally, with no evidence of local transmission in Arizona. The overall risk to the public remains low, according to the Arizona Department of Health Services. This brings the state's total mpox cases in 2026 to 34, including both Clade I and II infections. Health officials are conducting contact tracing and monitoring exposed individuals to prevent further spread. Clade II has been circulating in the U.S. since 2022, while Clade I cases remain uncommon. Vaccination is recommended for those at increased risk, and anyone with symptoms should isolate and seek testing.
Original source text (English)
Arizona health officials have confirmed the state's first travel-related case of Clade I mpox, a strain of the virus more commonly found in parts of Africa, according to the Arizona Department of Health Services. The infected Arizona resident contracted the virus while traveling outside the United States. ADHS said there is currently no evidence the case has led to local transmission in Arizona, and the overall risk to the general public remains low. State and local health officials are conducting contact tracing and monitoring individuals who may have been exposed to help prevent additional infections. Mpox is caused by the monkeypox virus and spreads primarily through close, personal contact. Health officials say transmission can occur through direct contact with a rash, sores, or bodily fluids, as well as through shared bedding, towels, clothing, and prolonged close contact with an infected person. Casual interactions in public places such as stores, offices, or airplanes are considered unlikely to spread the virus. Arizona has reported 34 mpox cases so far in 2026, including both Clade I and Clade II infections. Clade II has been circulating in Arizona and across the United States since 2022, while Clade I cases remain uncommon in the U.S. According to ADHS, symptoms of mpox include a new or unexplained rash that may appear as pimples or blisters, along with fever, headache, muscle aches, swollen lymph nodes, chills, exhaustion, and respiratory symptoms. People with weakened immune systems may face a higher risk of severe illness. Health officials continue to recommend vaccination for people at increased risk of exposure. The two-dose JYNNEOS vaccine can help prevent mpox and reduce the severity of illness. Anyone who develops symptoms should avoid close contact with others, isolate at home, and contact a healthcare provider for testing. ADHS officials say they will continue monitoring mpox activity statewide and provide updates as new information becomes available. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

“贝努埃没有确诊的 猴痘 病例”——卫生专员 - 尼日利亚《每日邮报》

‘No confirmed Mpox case in Benue’ – Health Commissioner - Daily Post Nigeria
新闻简报贝努埃州卫生部驳斥了该州确诊 猴痘 病例的谣言,并澄清称,虽然 Konshisha 地方政府区的 Tyokula Achihi 住宅报告了疑似 猴痘 病例,但尚未确认 猴痘 暴发 病例。卫生专员Paul Ogwuche博士表示,卫生部立即启动了监测系统,部署人员进行实地评估,并收集临床样本进行实验室检测。他强调,皮疹和发烧可能是由各种医疗状况引起的,需要实验室确认 猴痘 诊断。敦促公众保持冷静,向卫生机构报告症状,并避免传播未经证实的信息。该部在所有 23 个地方政府区域保持活跃的 监测,并与国家和国际卫生合作伙伴合作,以确保及时发现和应对。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 25 日。事件日期:未明确说明,但声明于周六发布。
地点与对象
尼日利亚贝努埃州,特别是 Korinya、Konshisha 地方政府区。受影响人群:所提供的证据中未报告。
事件
贝努埃州卫生与公众服务部驳回了该州确诊 猴痘 病例的传言。Tyokula Achihi Residence 报告了疑似 暴发,但尚未确认 猴痘 暴发。该事件正在调查中。
规模、影响与风险
没有确诊的 猴痘 病例、死亡或暴露报告。该部启动了 监测 系统,并部署人员进行实地评估和实验室测试。风险级别:提供的证据中未报告。
应对、状态与不确定性
该部启动了 监测 系统,并部署了疾病 监测 和通报官员进行病例调查和样本收集。实验室测试正在进行中。建议公众向卫生机构报告发烧并出现皮疹的情况,并避免传播未经证实的信息。与国家疾病预防控制中心、联邦卫生部、世界卫生组织和其他合作伙伴保持合作。目前情况正在调查中,尚未确认 暴发。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

‘No confirmed Mpox case in Benue’ – Health Commissioner - Daily Post Nigeria

News Brief
The Benue State Ministry of Health has refuted rumours of a confirmed Mpox case in the state, clarifying that while a suspected outbreak was reported at Tyokula Achihi Residence in Konshisha Local Government Area, no Mpox outbreak has been confirmed. Health Commissioner Dr Paul Ogwuche stated that the ministry immediately activated its surveillance system, deployed officers for on-the-ground assessment, and collected clinical samples for laboratory testing. He emphasized that skin rashes and fever can result from various medical conditions, requiring laboratory confirmation for Mpox diagnosis. The public is urged to remain calm, report symptoms to health facilities, and avoid spreading unverified information. The ministry maintains active surveillance across all 23 Local Government Areas and collaborates with national and international health partners to ensure prompt detection and response.
Original source text (English)
Home News Politics Metro Sport Entertainment Opinion Education Health Interview Daily Post Ghana Connect with us Daily Post Nigeria ‘No confirmed Mpox case in Benue’ – Health Commissioner Home News Politics Metro Entertainment Sport Health ‘No confirmed Mpox case in Benue’ – Health Commissioner Published on July 25, 2026 By Ejembi Ogwuche The Benue State Ministry of Health and Human Services has dismissed rumours making the rounds that there is a reported case of monkeypox in the state. This was contained in a statement on Saturday by the Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr Paul Ogwuche. He said the ministry had become aware of reports circulating on social media regarding a suspected Mpox outbreak at Tyokula Achihi Residence, Korinya, Konshisha Local Government Area. Dr Ogwuche said immediately the information came to the ministry’s attention, it activated its surveillance system in line with established public health protocols. According to him, “The Director of Public Health was directed to liaise with the State Epidemiologist, who in turn immediately deployed the Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha Local Government Area to the reported location for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation and collection of appropriate clinical samples for laboratory testing.” He said that, at this stage, no Mpox outbreak had been confirmed in Benue State. “It is important to emphasise that skin rashes and fever can result from several medical conditions, and laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made,” he said. Dr Ogwuche said further, “Our disease surveillance system remains active across all 23 Local Government Areas, and we maintain close collaboration with the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), the Federal Ministry of Health, the World Health Organization (WHO), and other development partners to ensure prompt detection and response to any public health event.” The commissioner appealed to members of the public to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information capable of creating unnecessary panic. “We encourage anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes or blisters to report immediately to the nearest health facility for proper medical evaluation and to avoid close physical contact until assessed by healthcare professionals. “We commend members of the community for promptly reporting the situation, as early reporting is essential for effective disease surveillance and response,” he stated. “Should the results confirm Mpox or any other disease of public health importance, appropriate response measures will be implemented immediately in accordance with national guidelines,” he added. B routine priorityPublic-health news

Benue begins probe into suspected Mpox case - TheCable

News Brief
Benue State, Nigeria, has launched an investigation into a suspected Mpox case in Konshisha LGA following social media reports. Health Commissioner Paul Ogwuche confirmed no outbreak has been declared, emphasizing that laboratory testing is required for confirmation. The ministry has activated its disease surveillance system and deployed a disease surveillance officer to assess the situation and collect samples. Collaboration with national and international health authorities is ongoing. Residents are advised to seek medical attention for fever and rashes and refrain from spreading unverified information. The public will be informed as soon as investigation and lab results are available.
Original source text (English)
Stay connected via Google News The Benue ministry of health says it has commenced an investigation into reports of a suspected Mpox case in Konshisha LGA of the state. In a statement issued on Saturday, Paul Ogwuche, commissioner of health, said no outbreak has been confirmed in the state. Ogwuche said the ministry has activated its disease surveillance system after reports circulating on social media alleged a suspected Mpox outbreak at Tyokula Achihi in Konshisha LGA. Ogwuche said the director of public health had directed the state epidemiologist to deploy the disease surveillance and notification officer (DSNO) for Konshisha to conduct an on-the-ground assessment, investigate the suspected case and collect clinical samples for laboratory testing. Advertisement He cautioned that laboratory analysis is required before Mpox can be confirmed, noting that fever and skin rashes may be symptoms of several medical conditions. “Our disease surveillance system remains active across all 23 LGAs, and we maintain close collaboration with the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC), the Federal Ministry of Health, the World Health Organization (WHO), and other development partners to ensure prompt detection and response to any public health event,” Ogwuche said. The commissioner urged residents to refrain from spreading unverified information capable of causing unnecessary panic. Advertisement He advised anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes and blisters to seek immediate medical attention at the nearest health facility. “We commend members of the community for promptly reporting the situation, as early reporting is essential for effective disease surveillance and response,” the commissioner said. “The ministry will continue to keep the public informed as soon as the investigation and laboratory findings become available. Should the results confirm Mpox or any other disease of public health importance, appropriate response measures will be implemented immediately in accordance with national guidelines.”

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Benue Investigates Suspected Mpox Case in Konshisha LGA - Punch Newspapers

News Brief
The Benue State Government in Nigeria is investigating a suspected Mpox case in Konshisha Local Government Area. Health Commissioner Dr. Paul Ogwuche confirmed the investigation and emphasized that laboratory confirmation is required for diagnosis. The ministry has activated its surveillance system, deployed officials to the area, and collected samples for testing. No outbreak has been confirmed yet. The public is urged to remain calm, report symptoms to health facilities, and avoid spreading unverified information. The state is collaborating with national and international health organizations to ensure prompt detection and response.
Original source text (English)
Discover moreInfectious DiseasesMedical Literature & ResourcesAfricans & DiasporagovernmentPoliticsGovernmentHealthpublic healthGeographic ReferenceNEWS ▼ × ×search Advertise with us Sunday, July 26, 2026 Most Widely Read Newspaper Subscribe: Punch E-Paper Subscribe: Punch E-Paper Home News Featured 2026 World Cup Metro Plus Business Sports HealthWise Editorial Columns Videos Continuation From Print PunchNG Menu: Politics Spice Special Features Education Sex & Relationship Interview Columns Opinion Advertise with us Benue investigates suspected Mpox case July 25, 2026 7:12 pm By John Charles Kindly share this story: The Benue State Government has commenced investigations into a suspected case of Mpox reported in Konshisha Local Government Area of the state. The Commissioner for Health and Human Services, Dr Paul Ogwuche, disclosed this on Saturday while responding to reports of a suspected case at the Tyokula Achihi residence in Korinya, Konshisha LGA. Ogwuche said the ministry immediately activated its surveillance system in line with established public health protocols and deployed officials to the area. In a statement made available to journalists in Makurdi, the commissioner urged residents not to jump to conclusions whenever skin rashes and fever occur, noting that the symptoms could result from several medical conditions. He stressed that laboratory confirmation was required before any case could be diagnosed as Mpox. The statement read in part, “The Director of Public Health was directed to liaise with the State Epidemiologist, who in turn immediately deployed the Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha Local Government Area to the reported location for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation and collection of appropriate clinical samples for laboratory testing. “At this stage, no Mpox outbreak has been confirmed in Benue State. It is important to emphasise that skin rashes and fever can result from several medical conditions, and laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made.” Related News Stop wasting your destiny, Oyedepo warns youths against betting Matawalle consoles victims of Zamfara terrorist attacks NDLEA arrests Lagos businessman over 3.3kg cocaine bound for UK The commissioner said the state disease surveillance system remained active across all 23 local government areas. He added that the ministry was working closely with the Nigeria Centre for Disease Control and Prevention, the Federal Ministry of Health, the World Health Organisation and other development partners to ensure prompt detection and response to public health events. Ogwuche urged members of the public to remain calm and avoid spreading unverified information capable of creating unnecessary panic. “We encourage anyone experiencing fever accompanied by unexplained rashes or blisters to report immediately to the nearest health facility for proper medical evaluation and to avoid close physical contact until assessed by healthcare professionals,” the statement added. The commissioner said the ministry would continue to keep the public informed as soon as the investigation and laboratory findings became available. He added that appropriate response measures would be implemented in line with national guidelines if the results confirmed Mpox or any other disease of public health importance. Konshisha Local Government Area, Benue State, Nigeria. Affected population: residents of Tyokula Achihi residence in Korinya, Konshisha LGA.
Event
The Benue State Government has commenced investigations into a suspected case of Mpox reported in Konshisha Local Government Area. The case is under investigation and not confirmed.
Scale, impact and risk
No confirmed cases of Mpox outbreak in Benue State. One suspected case reported. No deaths or exposures mentioned. Risk level not explicitly stated.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Benue State Ministry of Health has activated its surveillance system and deployed officials to the area. The Disease Surveillance and Notification Officer (DSNO) for Konshisha LGA has been deployed for on-the-ground assessment, case investigation, and collection of clinical samples for laboratory testing. Laboratory confirmation is required before any diagnosis of Mpox can be made. The state disease surveillance system remains active across all 23 local government areas.

查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 21 日。事件日期是指 2026 年西澳大利亚州报告的 猴痘 病例增加情况。

澳大利亚西澳大利亚州。受影响的人群包括同性恋、双性恋和男男性行为者 (GBMSM),以及异性恋男性和女性。

西澳大利亚州确认 猴痘 病例增加。西澳大利亚州卫生部报告称,2026年的病例数已超过2025年和2024年。
