B routine priorityPublic-health news

Authorities Warn Mpox Cases Are On The Rise In Western Australia - Star Observer

News Brief
Western Australian authorities are warning of a rise in Mpox cases, with 36 confirmed cases in 2026 already surpassing the 28 cases reported in all of 2025. The majority of cases have affected the queer community, particularly gay, bisexual, and men who have sex with men, though infections have also been identified in heterosexual men and women. The Western Australia Department of Health is urging residents to recognize symptoms, seek medical advice promptly, and practice safe sex. Free Mpox vaccinations are available to eligible higher-risk groups, including GBMSM, sex workers, and others with increased exposure risk. The public is encouraged to ensure they are fully vaccinated with both doses.
Original source text (English)
Support Star Observer Fund Australia’s home of LGBTQIA+ journalism Support Us Subscribe Login Support Star ObserverFund Australia’s home of LGBTQ+ journalism Support Us Subscribe News International National News New South Wales News Victorian News Queensland News Entertainment Movies & TV Celebrity Drag Stage Music Gaming Books The Internet Directory Archives Scene Photos In Print Subscribe Advertise News International National News New South Wales News Victorian News Queensland News Entertainment Movies & TV Celebrity Drag Stage Music Gaming Books The Internet Directory Archives Scene Photos In Print Subscribe Advertise News Entertainment What’s on Trending Directory News Entertainment What’s on Trending Directory About Advertise Scene Photos In Print Contact Terms & Conditions About Advertise Scene Photos In Print Contact Terms & Conditions Search CLEAR Search Authorities Warn Mpox Cases Are On The Rise In Western Australia News Western Australia Michael James July 21, 2026 The Western Australia community is being warned to be on the alert after an increase in cases of Mpox was reported in the state. Authorities from the Department Of Health in Western Australia issued the update overnight. They warn the current cases to day in 2026 have exceeded all of those in 2025. Mpox on the rise in Western Australia According to Western Australia Department Of Health there have been 36 confirmed cases of Mpox in 2026 so far. Comparatively in the whole of 2025 there were just 28 confirmed cases and just 23 cases in 2024 in the state. The government identified that whilst the cases effected both gay and bisexual men as well as heterosexual men and women, the majority of the cases have effected the queer community. “While the majority of cases have been in gay, bisexual and men who have sex with men (GBMSM), several infections have also been identified in heterosexual men and women this year” the government said in a statement overnight. They warned “Western Australians to be aware of the signs and symptoms of mpox and to seek medical advice if they develop symptoms.” Director of the Communicable Disease Control Directorate, Dr Paul Armstrong reminded the public of the importance of safe sex, including the use of condoms, as well as ensuring early testing and getting vaccinated. “While most infections continue to occur in specific higher-risk groups, mpox can affect anyone who has close physical contact with an infected person,” Dr Armstrong said. “It’s important that anyone who develops symptoms, particularly an unexplained rash or skin lesions, seeks medical advice promptly and avoids close contact with others until they have been assessed.” “We are encouraging people who are eligible for vaccination to ensure they are fully protected by receiving both doses of the vaccine.” “Vaccination is recommended for people at higher risk of exposure, including eligible gay, bisexual and other men who have sex with men, sex workers and other people whose sexual practices place them at increased risk of mpox infection. A Medicare Card is not required for a free vaccination.” Earlier this year Thorne Harbour Health in Melbourne warned of a spike of Mpox cases across multiple states in Australia, reporting over 100 confirmed cases nationally. “While our communities have done well to keep mpox cases down, the reality is that it hasn’t disappeared entirely. Unfortunately, this is the time of year when we have seen a spike in cases in both the 2022 and 2024 outbreak,” said Caleb Hawk, THH’s Deputy Director of Health Promotion and Community Engagement at the time. The first confirmed case of Mpox was detected back in May 2022, since then cases have fluctuated, with no cases between November 2022 and May 2023 when two more cases were detected in Sydney. More cases were reported in Victoria in April 2024, with numbers continuing to rise later that year with WHO declaring Mpox a “Global Health Emergency” in August 2024. In July 2025 a second case of a rare strain of the virus was detected in Brisbane, as overall numbers starting to decline the public was still encouraged to continue vaccinations. Mpox vaccinations remain free and available, regardless of Medicare or visa status. For more information about how to get the mpox vaccination, head here. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

亚利桑那州卫生部确认出境美国居民感染 猴痘 变种病例 - KJZZ

Arizona Department of Health confirms case of mpox variant in resident who traveled out of U.S. - KJZZ
新闻简报亚利桑那州卫生官员已确认一名曾在美国境外旅行的居民感染 Clade-1 猴痘 变种病例。亚利桑那州卫生服务部的 Joel Terriques 博士表示,该病例于本月报告,与旅行有关,但没有当地 传播 的证据。罕见的 Clade-1 变种与亚利桑那州和美国流行的更常见的 Clade-2 不同,会引起类似的症状,包括皮疹、发烧、头痛和淋巴结肿大。官员们强调,由于病例罕见且缺乏并发症,普通民众面临的总体风险仍然极低。该州尚未发现进一步的传播。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发布于 2026 年 7 月 24 日;事件发生于 2026 年 7 月。
地点与对象
美国亚利桑那州;曾到美国境外旅行的亚利桑那州居民

亚利桑那州卫生服务部确认一名曾出国旅行的居民患有 Clade-1 猴痘 变种病例。该病例与旅行有关,没有本地传播的证据。

单一确诊病例;无明显并发症;一般人群的总体风险极低。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Arizona Department of Health confirms case of mpox variant in resident who traveled out of U.S. - KJZZ

News Brief
Arizona health officials have confirmed a case of the Clade-1 mpox variant in a resident who traveled outside the U.S. According to Dr. Joel Terriques of the Arizona Department of Health Services, the case was reported this month and is travel-related with no evidence of local transmission. The rare Clade-1 variant, distinct from the more common Clade-2 circulating in Arizona and the U.S., causes similar symptoms including rash, fever, headaches, and swollen lymph nodes. Officials emphasize the overall risk to the general population remains extremely low due to the case's rarity and lack of complications. No further spread has been detected in the state.
Original source text (English)

Arizona Department of Health confirms case of mpox variant in resident who traveled out of U.S. KJZZ | By Kathy Ritchie Published July 24, 2026 at 10:02 AM MST Facebook Threads LinkedIn Bluesky Email Cynthia S. Goldsmith, Russell Regnery/Centers for Disease Control and Prevention An electron microscopic image depicted a monkeypox virion. The state Department of Health Services says it has confirmed a case of a particular variant of mpox — known as Clade-1 — in an Arizona resident traveling outside the U.S.Dr. Joel Terriques is with DHS. He says they were notified this month and there’s no evidence of the illness spreading in the state.“Based on the rarity of this clade 1 mpox case and the fact that it was travel related with no significant complications, the overall risk for the general population is extremely low," Terriques said. There are two types of mpox — Clade 1 and the more common Clade 2 — which has been spreading across Arizona and the U.S. since 2022. Both types cause similar symptoms, including rash, fever, headaches and swollen lymph nodes. B routine priorityPublic-health news

ARIZONA DEPT. OF HEALTH: CONFIRMS ARIZONA’S FIRST CASE OF CLADE I MPOX; RISK TO GENERAL PUBLIC REMAINS LOW - news.cgtn.com

News Brief
The Arizona Department of Health has confirmed the state's first case of Clade I mpox, emphasizing that the risk to the general public remains low. This marks a significant public health development, though specific details about the patient, transmission, or response measures have not been disclosed. Health authorities are likely monitoring the situation closely to prevent further spread. The confirmation comes amid ongoing global efforts to monitor and manage mpox cases, particularly concerning different clades. Public health officials are expected to provide more information as the investigation progresses.

Latest primary election results; 1st 'Clade I' Mpox case confirmed in Arizona | Nightly Roundup

News Brief
Arizona has confirmed its first case of Mpox involving Clade I, a different strain of the virus. The case was reported by FOX10Phoenix.com on July 22, 2026. This marks a significant development as Clade I is distinct from the more commonly circulating strains. Public health authorities have not provided additional details regarding the patient's condition, potential exposures, or the ongoing response. The confirmation highlights the importance of continued surveillance and monitoring for various strains of the Mpox virus.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

以身作则:尼日利亚医学生群体对猴痘疫苗的态度与接受度 - 生物通

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报2026 年 7 月 21 日发表在《科学报告》上的一项横断面研究调查了尼日利亚西北部 301 名医学生对 猴痘 疫苗 的态度和可接受性。该调查在巴耶罗大学(卡诺)和艾哈迈杜贝洛大学(扎里亚)进行,发现 疫苗 的可接受性很高(81.4%),但存在巨大的经济障碍。虽然 67.4% 的学生了解 疫苗,但只有 31.6% 的学生持积极态度。统计分析表明,负担能力/可及性、感知重要性和副作用担忧独立影响支付意愿。该研究建议采取有针对性的干预措施,解决成本、知识差距和安全沟通问题,以提高这一高风险群体的 疫苗接种 率。
查看新闻五要素
时间
发表于2026-07-21;研究在尼日利亚西北部地区进行。
地点与对象
尼日利亚,特别是卡诺州立大学(巴耶罗大学)和卡杜纳州立大学(扎里亚艾哈迈杜贝洛大学);医学生从100级到600级。
事件
一项横断面调查,调查尼日利亚医学生 猴痘 疫苗 的态度、可接受性和支付意愿;301 名参与者作出回应。
规模、影响与风险
203名学生(67.4%)知道猴痘 疫苗;95人(31.6%)持积极态度;245 人(81.4%)愿意接种疫苗。经济障碍是影响疫苗接种意愿的首要问题。
应对、状态与不确定性
研究确定了影响支付意愿的关键因素:负担能力/可及性(AOR=0.379)、感知重要性(AOR=0.444)和副作用担忧(AOR=0.489)。建议采取针对负担能力、知识和安全信息的干预措施来提高吸收率。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
以身作则:尼日利亚医学生群体对猴痘疫苗的态度与接受度 - 生物通
News Brief
A cross-sectional study published in Scientific Reports on July 21, 2026, investigated mpox vaccine attitudes and acceptability among 301 medical students in northwestern Nigeria. Conducted at Bayero University (Kano) and Ahmadu Bello University (Zaria), the survey found high vaccine acceptability (81.4%) but significant economic barriers. While 67.4% of students were aware of the vaccine, only 31.6% held positive attitudes. Statistical analysis revealed that affordability/accessibility, perceived importance, and side effect concerns independently influenced willingness to pay. The study recommends targeted interventions addressing cost, knowledge gaps, and safety communication to enhance vaccination rates among this high-risk group.
Original source text (Chinese)

“畅读会员”点此登录查看原文链接Article Open access 摘要猴痘在多国的暴发促使世界卫生组织将其宣布为国际关注的公共卫生紧急事件。鉴于尼日利亚近期出现的病例以及医学生可能面临的风险,本研究调查了尼日利亚西北部医学生对猴痘疫苗的认知、态度、接受度及支付意愿。研究在尼日利亚西北部的卡诺州贝约大学和卡杜纳州扎里亚的艾哈迈杜·贝洛大学,对100级至600级的医学生进行了横断面调查。通过便利抽样方法共获得了301份回复。数据通过在线自填问卷收集,其中包括6项社会人口学资料、5项关于认知的题目、6项关于态度的题目、5项关于接受度的题目,以及6项关于支付意愿的题目。数据以频率和百分比的形式呈现。研究采用卡方检验和二元逻辑回归分析影响支付意愿的因素。共有301名医学生参与了这项研究。其中大多数人的年龄在21至25岁之间(n?=?176,58.6%),性别为男性(n?=?199,66.1%)。贝约大学贡献了最多的受访者(n?=?184,61.1%),而300级的学生占比最低(n?=?25,8.3%)。在宗教和种族方面,大多数参与者为穆斯林(n?=?262,87.0%)和豪萨人(n?=?168,55.8%)。在301名受访者中,203人(67.4%,95%置信区间:62.1%?72.6%)了解猴痘疫苗,95人(31.6%)对接种疫苗持积极态度。疫苗的接受度很高,有245人(81.4%,95%置信区间:77%?85.6%)愿意接种该疫苗。疫苗的支付意愿与年龄组(χ2 (3)?=?13.839,p?=?0.003)和学习阶段(χ2 (5)?=?15.603,p?=?0.008)存在显著关联。二元逻辑回归分析出三个影响支付意愿的独立因素:对疫苗可负担性和可获得性的认知(AOR?=?0.379,95%置信区间:0.204–0.704,p?=?0.002)、对疫苗重要性的认知(AOR?=?0.444,95%置信区间:0.275–0.719,p?=?0.001),以及对副作用的担忧(AOR?=?0.489,95%置信区间:0.244–0.979,p?=?0.043)。尽管疫苗的接受度很高,但经济障碍仍是尼日利亚西北部医学生接种猴痘疫苗面临的主要问题。当疫苗广泛可用时,需要采取针对可负担性、疫苗知识普及和安全信息传递的干预措施,以提高人们的支付意愿并促进疫苗的接种。欢迎下载最佳的病毒清除验证和细胞库检测方案>>>领 取 打赏 展开全文 开通VIP 享海量文章 全站开放获取+解锁超过 200万篇新闻全文+每日更新 2000+篇,学术前沿动态全时追踪! View five news elements
Time
Published on 2026-07-21; Study conducted in Nigeria's northwestern region.
Location and population
Nigeria, specifically Kano State (Bayero University) and Kaduna State (Ahmadu Bello University, Zaria); medical students from 100 to 600 level.
Event
A cross-sectional survey investigating mpox vaccine attitudes, acceptability, and willingness to pay among Nigerian medical students; 301 participants responded.
Scale, impact and risk
203 students (67.4%) were aware of mpox vaccine; 95 (31.6%) had positive attitudes; 245 (81.4%) were willing to vaccinate. Economic barriers were the primary issue affecting vaccination willingness.
Response, status and uncertainty
Study identified key factors influencing payment willingness: affordability/accessibility (AOR=0.379), perceived importance (AOR=0.444), and side effect concerns (AOR=0.489). Interventions targeting affordability, knowledge, and safety information are recommended to improve uptake.
